**Deliverable:** implemented BACKLOG.md "Phase 7 authoring aids" final remaining slice — image paste + drag-and-drop — per Session 57's plan (`docs/planning/2026-07-09-image-paste-plan.md`), following `docs/methodology/workstreams/DEVELOPMENT_WORKSTREAM.md`. **BACKLOG.md "Phase 7 authoring aids" is now fully complete.**

**Kickoff:** operator picked "Implement image paste (Phase 7)" via `AskUserQuestion` from a 4-candidate list (the others: fix `POSIT-COMPARISON.md` staleness, clear `npm audit` advisories properly, something else). The plan's 3 open questions (§3 Q1/Q2/Q3) then resolved via a second `AskUserQuestion` round before any code: **Q1 destination = `images/` subfolder** (operator's own choice, differing from the plan's recommended no-subfolder default); **Q2 drag-and-drop bundled into v1** (the plan's own recommendation, adding L4 to the declared layer contract); **Q3 filename trusts `DataTransferFile.name` when present/non-empty, generates otherwise** (the plan's own recommendation).

**L1 (`df3bd8f`) — pure core, strict TDD:** `src/core/image-paste.ts` built up through `test/unit/image-paste.test.ts` (15 tests): `extensionForMimeType` (a small MIME→extension map), `deriveImageName` (Q3's naming logic — trusts a present/non-empty file name, splitting a real extension when present or guessing one from the MIME type when absent; generates `"image"` + a MIME-guessed extension only when the name itself is absent/empty; a `".png"`-style dotfile name is treated as a base name, not an empty one), `resolveNonCollidingName` (mirrors VS Code's own `newFilePathGenerator.ts` collision-avoidance loop, MIT, read for precedent not copied), `buildImageRelativePath` (Q1's `images/` convention), and `buildImagePasteInsertText` (POSIX-normalized Markdown reference). Each discriminating behavior shown RED for the correct reason before GREEN; the collision-avoidance loop additionally break-revert-proven.

**L2 (`5b0e26c`) — paste adapter:** `src/providers/image-paste.ts` `QmdImagePasteEditProvider` registered via `vscode.languages.registerDocumentPasteEditProvider({language:"quarto"}, ...)` — zero `package.json` contribution, matching `providers/workspace-symbols.ts`'s precedent. Scans the paste `DataTransfer` for a file-backed `image/*` entry, reads its bytes, resolves a non-colliding name against `images/`'s real directory listing (one `workspace.fs.readDirectory` call, keeping L1's `exists` predicate synchronous as designed), and returns a `DocumentPasteEdit` with a `WorkspaceEdit.createFile` + the Markdown insert text. No file-backed image → returns `undefined`, falls through to default paste. Wired in `extension.ts`.

**L3 (`2763bad`) — integration tests that refined the plan's own D1 disclosure:** empirically discovered (a throwaway experimental test, confirmed before committing to the technique) that `vscode.DataTransfer`/`DataTransferItem` do not runtime-validate that stored/iterated values are genuine class instances — `findImageFile` only ever calls `.asFile()` duck-typed, so a hand-built object satisfying the (interface, not class-with-hidden-state) `DataTransferFile` shape flows through the REAL registered provider exactly like a real OS paste would. This let the test suite exercise the real byte-read + write + collision-avoidance path end-to-end via `vscode.workspace.applyEdit` and a real on-disk `mkdtemp` fixture — not just mime-routing/fallback coverage, substantially narrower than the plan's original "unverifiable by `@vscode/test-electron`" framing. `PROJECT_LEARNINGS.md` Learning #66 appended (65→66, ascending order confirmed). The collision-avoidance-against-a-real-directory-listing and real-byte-content assertions both break-revert-proven against the real registered provider (not a reimplementation).

**L4 (`5942108`) — drag-and-drop mirror, per Q2:** refactored the shared write/naming logic into `buildImageResult`, wrapped by both `QmdImagePasteEditProvider` (behavior unchanged) and the new `QmdImageDropEditProvider` (`vscode.languages.registerDocumentDropEditProvider`, differing only in registration call and callback signature — `position` instead of `ranges`/`context`, exactly as the plan predicted). Both registered in one `registerImagePasteFeature` call; no new `package.json` contribution. 3 new integration tests (mime-routing, non-file-backed fallback, and a real file-backed write-through-`applyEdit` proof); the `additionalEdit` wiring break-revert-proven.

**Full verify matrix:** 610 unit (+15) / 215 integration (+8) / clean 42-file `.vsix` (unchanged file count — zero size/manifest impact, confirmed via `npm run package`'s file listing, per plan §5) / `npm run check-types` clean — run at every layer boundary, not deferred to the end.

**Docs:** `BACKLOG.md`'s "Phase 7 authoring aids" item checked off with the full 4-layer summary — Phase 7 is now fully complete. `CHANGELOG.md` dated entry added. `PROJECT_LEARNINGS.md` Learning #66 appended. `docs/POSIT-COMPARISON.md`'s image-paste row updated from "not implemented" to "present," and its two "At a Glance" category counts corrected ("True parity in absence" 2→1, "We're ahead" 2→3) — since Posit's own source-editor `.qmd` paste-image remains an open, unimplemented feature request, this ships ahead of Posit, not parity catch-up.

### What Session 55 Did — 2026-07-09
**Deliverable:** a plan document for BACKLOG.md "Up Next" item #7 — graphviz (`{dot}`) diagram rendering — `docs/planning/2026-07-09-graphviz-dot-rendering-plan.md`. Not code; implementation is a separate future session.

**Why a planning session, not implementation:** unlike items #4/#5/#6 (mechanical/declarative extensions with no open design question), this item had no pre-declared layer contract and a genuine choice between two viable WASM Graphviz renderer packages plus an unverified CSP/license assumption carried forward since Session 16. Per `SESSION_RUNNER.md`'s Vertical Slice Sessions Gate (a), that means a planning session first — the same pattern items #1-3 followed (Sessions 44/46/48 planned, 45/47/49-51 implemented).

**Research, grounded empirically (not from documentation/memory):** downloaded `@viz-js/viz` (v3.28.0) and `@hpcc-js/wasm-graphviz` (v1.24.1) directly from the real npm registry, extracted their actual published tarballs, and ran both to completion in Node (`node -e`), producing real Graphviz-generated SVG from `digraph { a -> b }` — genuine execution, not research-agent summarization. Grepped both bundles' real code for `eval(`/`new Function(`/`WebAssembly.instantiate`/`WebAssembly.instantiateStreaming`: neither calls plain JS eval, both call `WebAssembly.instantiate`, empirically locking the CSP requirement to `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` only (not the broader `'unsafe-eval'` the original Session-16 guess had left ambiguous). Fetched Graphviz's own official license page directly and caught a real error in this plan's own first instinct: Graphviz is EPL-**2.0**, not EPL-1.0. Measured real file sizes (`@viz-js/viz`'s `dist/viz-global.js`: 1.33 MB raw / 480 KB gzip; `@hpcc-js/wasm-graphviz`'s `dist/index.js`: 814 KB raw / 634 KB gzip) rather than estimating.

**Decision: recommend `@viz-js/viz`, vendoring `dist/viz-global.js`.** Both candidates work equally well technically; the tiebreaker is that `@viz-js/viz` ships an official classic-`<script>`/UMD global build that reuses this project's *already-proven* KaTeX/Mermaid vendoring pattern exactly (`globalThis.Viz`, no new webview-loading technique), whereas `@hpcc-js/wasm-graphviz` is ESM-only and would require introducing `<script type="module">` webview loading in the same slice as a new CSP directive.

**One open question deliberately NOT resolved by this plan (§9 Q1):** vendoring `viz-global.js` ships a compiled copy of Graphviz — EPL-2.0, a weak-copyleft license — inside this project's MIT-licensed `.vsix`. Legally uncomplicated to redistribute in object-code form with proper notice (many MIT/Apache projects bundle EPL binaries), but it is the first time this project would do so, and `CLAUDE.md`/`NOTICE` both currently frame the project as unqualified-MIT. Flagged for explicit operator sign-off via `AskUserQuestion` before implementation's L1 (vendoring) begins — not decided unilaterally here.

**Also confirmed, so a future implementer doesn't re-derive it:** `{dot}` detection (`core/diagram-regions.ts`/`findDiagramRegions`) needs zero changes — already correct, verified by re-reading it and its full passing test suite. The entire gap is in the render/CSP layer (`core/diagram-preview-html.ts`, `features/diagram-preview.ts`).

**A parallel research `Workflow`** (two candidate-research agents + a synthesis stage) was launched as a planned cross-check; the two research agents completed but the synthesis stage ran unusually long, so it was stopped via `TaskStop` once this session's own firsthand execution-based research was already complete and sufficient — the plan's conclusions rest on this session's direct verification, not on that workflow's output (disclosed, not silently omitted).

**Docs:** `BACKLOG.md` item #7 (both the ranked-list entry and the "Phase 7 authoring aids" entry) annotated PLANNED with a pointer to the plan and a summary of its corrected CSP/license conclusions — left as an open checkbox (`[ ]`), not shipped. `PROJECT_LEARNINGS.md` Learning #63 appended (checked ascending order immediately after the edit — 62→63, correct): carried-forward "needs X" assumptions across multiple handoffs are hypotheses, not facts, and should be verified empirically at the planning session rather than trusted because they've been repeated.

**L2 adapter + new test infrastructure (`41593e9`) — the adapter was also test-driven:** wrote `test/integration/suite/workspace-symbols.test.ts` FIRST and ran the full integration suite before any adapter code existed — confirmed RED for the right reason (0 matches, not 1). Implemented `src/providers/workspace-symbols.ts` `registerWorkspaceSymbolsProvider` (a `vscode.WorkspaceSymbolProvider` that calls `vscode.workspace.findFiles("**/*.qmd", ...)`, opens each match via `vscode.workspace.openTextDocument` — which transparently returns the SAME document instance if already open/dirty, no extra branch needed — parses it with the existing `buildOutline`, flattens with `flattenOutline`, filters with `matchesWorkspaceQuery`, and maps to `vscode.SymbolInformation` using the same heading→`String`/cell→`Function` kind mapping `providers/outline.ts` already uses for consistency). Wired in `extension.ts`; no `package.json` contribution needed (registering a workspace symbol provider needs no manifest entry, matching `providers/outline.ts`'s precedent).

Fixed by adding `launchArgs` to `test/integration/runTest.ts`, opening the existing `test/fixtures/project` fixture (already used by `render-project.test.ts`, 2 real `.qmd` files: `index.qmd` + `chapters/chapter1.qmd`) as the shared workspace folder for the WHOLE integration run (one Extension Development Host, shared by every suite). ### What Session 52 Did — 2026-07-09
**Deliverable:** implemented BACKLOG.md "Up Next" item #4 — the run-cell command family completion — following `docs/methodology/workstreams/DEVELOPMENT_WORKSTREAM.md`.

**No kickoff question:** unlike BACKLOG item #3's three tracks, item #4 had no flagged open question — its own BACKLOG framing (4 exact command names, "bind default keybindings across the full resulting 9-command family, in the SAME session... mechanical extension of core/cells.ts/execution-delegate.ts") was fully actionable as written.

**Implementation, 4 strict-TDD vertical slices (`src/features/execution.ts`):**
1. `quarto.runSelectedLines` (`c097574`) — delegates the live editor selection, or the current line when the selection is empty, instead of the whole cell. Requires the cursor to sit inside a cell (to resolve the delegate language); shows the same "place the cursor inside a code cell" message as the existing family when it doesn't.
2. `quarto.runNextCell` (`595942b`) — runs the first cell after the cursor (from prose OR from inside a cell — a single `c.startLine > line` filter handles both cases with no branch, since a cell's own bounds exclude it from a strict comparison against any line inside it) and moves the cursor into it, so repeated invocation steps forward.
3. `quarto.runPreviousCell` (`95be370`) — symmetric backward version (`c.endLine < line`, take the last match).
4. `quarto.runCellsBelow` (`ef79ee3`) — symmetric to the existing `runCellsAbove`, running every cell strictly below the cursor.

Each slice: integration test(s) written first, full suite run and confirmed RED for the right reason (`command '...' not found`), then implemented, then confirmed GREEN. No new `core/` functions needed — all 4 reuse `findAllCells`/`findCellAtPosition` (`core/cells.ts`) and `pickDelegate` (`core/execution-delegate.ts`), matching the existing `runCellsAbove`/`runAllCells` precedent of adapter-only logic with integration-only coverage.

**Declarative layer (`3d5a60b`, `package.json`):** 4 new `contributes.commands` entries, plus keybindings across the FULL resulting 9-command family — including the 3 pre-existing commands (`runCellsAbove`/`runAllCells`/`insertCell`) that had never had a keybinding before this session (a gap this project's own `docs/POSIT-COMPARISON.md` research had already flagged). `ctrl+enter`/`shift+enter` (existing `runCell`/`runCellAndAdvance`) are unchanged. New: `ctrl+shift+enter` (Run Selected Line(s), in-cell-gated like the two Enter-family originals), `ctrl+alt+a`/`ctrl+alt+b` (Above/Below), `ctrl+alt+enter` (Run All), `ctrl+alt+n`/`ctrl+alt+p` (Next/Previous), `ctrl+alt+i` (Insert Cell) — all `editorLangId == quarto`-gated, none in-cell-gated except Run Selected Line(s) (the rest are meaningful regardless of cursor position). Deliberately avoided `ctrl+alt+up`/`ctrl+alt+down` for Next/Previous — VS Code's own stock keybindings claim those for multi-cursor add-above/below (`PROJECT_LEARNINGS.md` Learning #59).

**Verification:** `npm test` (557 unit, unchanged) / `npm run check-types` (clean) / `npm run test:integration` (200, +11) / `npm run package` (clean 40-file `.vsix`, unchanged file count — code + manifest only, no new media). Removed the local `.vsix` artifact after inspecting it (gitignored, not committed).

**Docs (`491d653`):** `BACKLOG.md` item #4 checked off with full implementation summary; a new Polish/deferred item files `docs/POSIT-COMPARISON.md`'s pre-existing, unrelated staleness (3 OTHER already-shipped items — YAML diagnostics, project-level render, the walkthrough — still listed as open gaps there) for a future documentation-accuracy session, not fixed here. `CHANGELOG.md` entry added. `docs/POSIT-COMPARISON.md`'s own run-cell-family entry corrected to reflect parity (11 real gaps, down from 12; the "At a Glance" table and "What This Suggests for Future Work" list item #4 both updated) — the one claim this session's own change directly falsified. `PROJECT_LEARNINGS.md` Learning #59 appended (checked ascending order after edit — 58→59, correct).

**Deliverable:** implemented Track B (`quarto.createProject`) of `docs/planning/2026-07-09-onboarding-walkthrough-plan.md` §3 (Session 48's plan), following `docs/methodology/workstreams/DEVELOPMENT_WORKSTREAM.md`, as one vertical-slice session per the plan's pre-declared 2-checkpoint contract.

**Kickoff (before any code):** two questions flagged by the plan (§7 Q2/Q3). Q2 (does `quarto create-project` auto-launch an editor?) was resolved empirically — spawned the real installed Quarto 1.7.33 CLI's `create-project` in an OS scratch directory: completed in 0.126s wall time, exited 0, wrote exactly the expected `_quarto.yml`/`.qmd` files, and the process list showed no new editor/window process before vs. after. Confirmed: no auto-launch, so the spawn call needs no `--no-open` flag handling or stdio isolation. Q3 (post-success behavior) was asked via `AskUserQuestion`: operator chose "Open as workspace (Recommended)" over "Reveal `index.qmd` in current window."

**L1 (core, `343598f`) — strict TDD:** `src/core/create-project-args.ts` `buildCreateProjectArgs(targetDir, type, title)` built up through `test/unit/create-project-args.test.ts`: (1) a default-type project's exact argv shape — RED confirmed (module didn't exist: `Failed to load url ../../src/core/create-project-args`), then implemented as a single-line `return ["create-project", targetDir, "--type", type, "--title", title]`; (2) the three other project types (website/book/manuscript, parameterized `it.each`) — passed immediately, same code path, disclosed as such; (3) an absolute-path-passthrough assertion (the target directory is never resolved/manipulated, mirroring `render-project.ts`'s D1 discipline) — passed immediately; (4) a title-containing-spaces assertion (stays one argv element — `child_process.spawn` with an argv array bypasses shell parsing entirely, so no manual quoting is needed or wanted) — passed immediately. `core/` confirmed `vscode`-free by grep.

**L2 (adapter+wiring+integration, `cdebaa4`) — the adapter was also test-driven:** wrote `test/integration/suite/create-project.test.ts` FIRST (command registration + a real-CLI happy-path case + a cancellation case) and ran the full integration suite before any adapter code existed — confirmed RED for the right reason (`Error: command 'quarto.createProject' not found`, all 3 new tests failing, the other 186 integration tests unaffected). Then implemented `src/features/create-project.ts` `registerCreateProjectFeature` (plan §3.3's exact shape: `showQuickPick` type → `showOpenDialog` parent folder → `showInputBox` name/title, any cancellation at any step aborts quietly → `resolveBinary()` reusing `render-project.ts`'s `QuartoNotFound` branch → spawn `quarto create-project <targetDir> --type <type> --title <title>` via `buildCreateProjectArgs` → on success, `vscode.commands.executeCommand("vscode.openFolder", vscode.Uri.file(targetDir))`), wired it in `extension.ts`, added one `package.json` command entry (`"Create Project"`, category `"Quarto"`). Re-ran the full integration suite: 189 passing (186 + 3 new), 0 failing — GREEN.

**Two dragons, both confirmed successfully:** (1) `showQuickPick`/`showOpenDialog` extend the reassign-in-`finally` monkey-patch stub technique with zero adjustment — closing the gap Session 49's Learning #56 explicitly left open ("a supporting data point, not proof"). (2) A genuinely new OS-temp-dir integration-test pattern (`fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), "quarto-ext-createproject-"))` per test, removed unconditionally in `afterEach`, confirmed never created inside the repo working tree — verified directly after the test run). (3) An unanticipated third dragon, not explicitly named by the plan: `vscode.openFolder` is invoked via `vscode.commands.executeCommand`, not a directly-stubbable namespace function, and a real invocation reloads the Extension Development Host window — which would abort the test run. Rather than taking the plan's own pre-authorized fallback ("assert the CLI's file-creation side effect only... document the open-folder step as verified by direct/manual launch instead"), this session generalized the SAME monkey-patch technique one level up: reassigned `vscode.commands.executeCommand` itself, intercepting only calls whose first argument is `"vscode.openFolder"` (capture the `Uri`, resolve without executing) and delegating every other command name — including the `quarto.createProject` invocation under test — to the original implementation. This let the test assert the exact resolved `Uri` without ever triggering a reload. `PROJECT_LEARNINGS.md` Learning #57 appended documenting all three findings.

**Verification, both checkpoint boundaries:** `npm test` (534 unit, +6) / `npm run check-types` (clean) / `npm run test:integration` (189, +3) / `npm run package` (clean 35-file `.vsix`, matching the established baseline). Confirmed no stray temp directories left in the repo tree or `$TMPDIR` after the integration run. Removed the local `.vsix` artifact after inspecting it (gitignored, not committed).

### Useful starting context
- **★ YAML schema diagnostics SHIPPED (Session 47, → Learning #54).** New always-on feature: opening a `_quarto.yml`/`_quarto.yaml` scans its `project:`/`website:`/`book:` blocks for keys the installed Quarto schema doesn't recognize and surfaces each as an Error `Diagnostic` (Problems panel + red squiggle). `src/core/yaml-schema.ts` `SchemaIndex.projectKeys(container)` resolves the closed key set incl. `book:`'s two-level `super`/`$ref` merge chain (`resolveClosedKeys`/`resolveClosedKeysRef`/`resolveClosedKeysObject` — per-branch `seenRefs` COPIES, no shared mutable set, no depth cap needed); `src/core/project-yaml.ts` `findProjectConfigKeyLines` is the pure whole-document scanner (bare, non-front-matter-fenced YAML) + the pure filename gate `isProjectConfigFileName` (exact basename, NOT a suffix match); `src/features/yaml-diagnostics.ts` `registerYamlDiagnosticsFeature` is the `DiagnosticCollection` adapter (this codebase's first use of `onDidCloseTextDocument`/`createDiagnosticCollection`), 350ms debounce, a per-URI generation counter guards a slow-first-load stale-overwrite race. **🔑 If touching this feature again, read it in full first — a 26-agent adversarial review found 2 HIGH-severity false positives (quoted keys, the filename-gate suffix-match) that survived careful TDD implementation; both are fixed, but the general lesson (always adversarially review a "flag unknown X" feature's handling of quoting/casing/encoding, not just its happy path) applies to any future extension.** **🔑 `vscode.workspace.onDidCloseTextDocument` does NOT fire for a real on-disk file document in the `@vscode/test-electron` integration host** (confirmed, extends Learning #3's boundary) — "clears on close" and any close-race behavior for THIS feature (or any future one using the same event) cannot be automated-tested here; verify by code trace + real interactive VS Code only. Known, documented, NOT-fixed gap: an anchored (`project: &p`) or quoted (`"project":`) container header disables scanning of that block (false negative, safe direction — `BACKLOG.md` Polish/deferred). 523 unit / 183 integration; clean 35-file `.vsix`.
- **★ Project-level render PLAN COMMITTED (Session 44, → Learning #51), READ THIS FIRST if implementing BACKLOG "Up Next" item #1:** `docs/planning/2026-07-09-project-level-render-plan.md`. A new `quarto.renderProject` ("Render Project") command: discover the project root (`_quarto.yml`/`_quarto.yaml`, walking up from the active file/workspace root, bounded at the owning `WorkspaceFolder`) then invoke `quarto render <root>` — **root passed EXPLICITLY as an argument, never a bare no-args invocation**. **🔑 Why explicit:** a live empirical test against the installed Quarto 1.7.33 CLI (independently re-verified by a second agent from scratch) found that bare `quarto render` run from a project SUBdirectory (no own `_quarto.yml`) silently renders ONLY that subdirectory's files while still inheriting the ancestor project's config — exit 0, a working success dialog, and a WRONG result (not a whole-project render). `quarto render <absolute-root-path>` from any cwd is the confirmed-reliable form. **🔑 A second trap:** Quarto reports `Output created: <path>` relative to the TARGET directory, not the spawning process's cwd — the new command's spawn MUST pin `cwd = root` or the "Open" success button resolves to the wrong file (plan Dragon D1). New pure `src/core/project.ts` (`findProjectRoot`, dependency-injected `exists` — mirrors `yaml-schema-source.ts`'s pure-core/impure-adapter split); this is the FIRST code in the repo to touch `vscode.workspace.workspaceFolders`/`getWorkspaceFolder`/`showWorkspaceFolderPick` (confirmed zero prior usage, twice, adversarially re-checked with a much wider grep net). Recommended as ONE vertical-slice session (4 checkpoint-committed layers: core discovery + unit tests; adapter/command/`package.json`/`extension.ts` wiring; new `test/fixtures/project/` fixture; integration tests) — plan §9 Q1 leaves splitting into 2 sessions as an explicit, equally-valid alternative. `src/features/render.ts`/`src/core/render-args.ts`/`src/quarto/cli.ts` are all REUSED, none require changes; `src/features/preview.ts` is confirmed (by reading it in full) NOT to generalize trivially to project scope — "Preview Project" stays its own future plan, not bundled here.
- **★ Posit feature-comparison doc SHIPPED (Session 42, → Learning #49), READ THIS if picking up any of its 6 suggested follow-ups:** `docs/POSIT-COMPARISON.md` — 31 rows, 7 categories + an "Additional Findings" section, built via a `Workflow` (5 parallel domain-research agents → 1 synthesis agent → 31 per-row adversarial-verify agents with Bash+WebFetch access). **🔑 The verify pass is the reusable pattern here, not just this doc's content:** 14/31 rows (45%) had a real defect at first draft — stale citations presented as current (a 2022 changelog entry for a feature since redesigned), a fabricated quote (cited text that didn't appear on the page when re-fetched), a wrong citation target (the right fact existed, but at a different URL than the one named), an overclaim on OUR OWN code (calling 1-level-deep completion "recursive"/"3+"), an undercount of a real gap (claimed 2 missing commands, verified 4), and one direction-reversal (claimed Posit has a keybinding advantage; verified Posit removed it in 2022 and we're actually ahead). **For any future competitive/comparison research: don't trust a first-draft synthesis agent's citations — a dedicated per-claim adversarial verify pass with tool access (grep our side, re-fetch their side) is where the real defects surface**, at roughly a 1-in-2 rate on claims spanning two codebases + external docs. The doc's own top section documents the AGPL-3.0 look-but-don't-copy boundary (Learning #1) and this session's exact grounding method. **Six concrete follow-ups proposed, priority-ordered, NONE added to BACKLOG.md yet** (deliberate scope boundary — promoting them is a future session's call): (1) YAML schema diagnostics/validation — our largest gap, builds on the existing `src/core/yaml-schema.ts` reader; (2) snippets (already in BACKLOG.md) + a getting-started walkthrough (NOT in BACKLOG.md — new finding); (3) project-level render (`_quarto.yml` discovery) — NOT in BACKLOG.md, new finding; (4) 4 missing run-cell commands (Run Selected Line(s)/Next/Previous Cell/Cells Below); (5) graphviz `{dot}` rendering (already in BACKLOG.md); (6) the Visual (WYSIWYG) editor — Posit's largest feature, explicitly out of this project's v1 scope, would need its own planning session if ever pursued. **Two places we're verifiably AHEAD of Posit, not just at parity:** per-format nested option completion is format-scoped on our side (Posit's own community docs admit their top-level suggestions are NOT format-filtered); and we bind default keybindings for Bold/Italic (Posit removed all three formatting keybindings in 2022 after a conflict and never restored them, confirmed via their current `package.json` + CHANGELOG).
- **★ `copyright` name-collision dedup fix SHIPPED (Session 41, → Learning #48).** `collectFields` (`src/core/yaml-schema.ts:973`) now keeps the RICHEST same-named `SchemaField` on a collision (`fieldRichness` = `children.length + values.length`), via a `Map<name, SchemaField>` that replaces content on `.set()` without moving position — so first-seen ORDER is preserved even when content changes. Fixes `copyright` (`document-attributes.yml`'s childless JATS-only one no longer beats `document-metadata.yml`'s real `year`/`holder`/`statement` object just because it iterates first); grep-verified 3 other duplicated document-key names (`logo`/`subject`/`footer`) tie in richness so they don't regress. User-visible at `format:\n  <fmt>:\n    copyright:\n      <here>` (b2-iii-key's existing children-navigation path) — now offers `year`/`holder`/`statement` where it silently offered nothing before. If touching `collectFields`/`perFormatSource`/name-collision handling again: re-grep the real schema for duplicated names first (`data[key]` entries sharing a `.name` across `schema/document-*` or `schema/cell-*` files) — don't assume `copyright` was the only instance. Tests: `test/unit/yaml-schema-index.test.ts` (65; +1, fixture mirrors the real collision with the poorer entry inserted first to reproduce the real JSON key order), `test/integration/suite/yaml.test.ts` (172; +1, against the REAL installed schema, break-revert-proven). One commit (`b03e705`).
- **★ Phase 6d-6+ (b2-iii-key) SHIPPED (Session 37, → Learning #44) — deep-nested per-format option KEY completion, READ THIS FIRST if doing b2-iii-value:** Under `format:\n  <fmt>:\n    <opt>:\n      <key>`, offer an object-valued option's sub-keys, one object level deep. **`src/core/yaml-context.ts`** `nestedParentPath` generalized from a bounded 2-level cap to an N-level ancestor walk (climbs any number of pure-mapping levels, returns the path only when the column-0 root is `format:` — schema-free, position ⊥ data). **`src/core/yaml-schema.ts`**: new pure `resolveObjectProperties` (walks `anyOf`/`ref`/`maybeArrayOf`/the `{schema: X}` indirection, `seenRefs`-cycle-guarded — **deliberately does NOT unwrap a bare/unconditional `arrayOf`**, see below), `childDescription` (nested description lookup, depth≤3), `objectChildren` (composes these, capped at exactly ONE object level — a child's own children are never computed); `SchemaField.children`; `toField` wires it in; `frontMatterKeys` gained the `parentPath.length>=3 && [0]==="format"` branch (factored `perFormatOptions` closure shared with the existing len===2 branch). `CURATED_FORMAT_OPTIONS` gained curated `code-tools`/`theme` children (offline fallback). **`src/providers/yaml.ts` UNCHANGED** (generic over `parentPath`, verified — like every prior nested slice). **🔑 Grounding beat the plan's own pseudocode:** mirroring the intended algorithm in a throwaway Python script against the REAL installed 1.7.33 schema needed FIVE unwrap forms (not the plan's three) to reach the grounded "40 object-valued options" fact exactly — bare `arrayOf` and the `{schema: X}` wrapper were both missing from the plan's §5.2 pseudocode. **🔑 THE REVIEW'S HEADLINE CATCH (Learning #44c):** a bare/unconditional `arrayOf` (e.g. `other-links`, `filters` — a sequence, NEVER a mapping) is NOT the same completion shape as `maybeArrayOf` (may be a single object OR array — a mapping IS valid there); unwrapping both the same way produced schema-invalid completions (`other-links:\n  text: …`). Fixed by removing the bare-`arrayOf` branch entirely — such an option now correctly offers nothing (sequence-item completion is out of scope, same as the detector's existing bail-on-`- `). Two more review fixes: a `!Array.isArray(properties)` guard (a malformed schema could otherwise emit index-named "0"/"1"/"2" garbage keys); and 3 new `childDescription` assertions (its resolved output was previously untested). All fixed test-first, all break-revert-proven (the `arrayOf` fix also break-revert-proven against the REAL schema). **🔑 Session recovery (FM #14):** orientation found a ghost-session gap — a prior instance of this session had committed ONLY the detector layer then vanished without closing out; the checkpoint-commit discipline meant nothing was lost, the continuing instance just picked up at layer 2. **For b2-iii-value:** trace first (most inline sub-values already work via the existing `valuesOfSchema` reuse), but ground+fix the 3 known `valuesOfSchema` gaps (2 from the plan + 1 newly found by this review — see ACTIVE TASK). Tests: `test/unit/yaml-schema-index.test.ts` (60; +11 incl. the arrayOf/description/malformed-array regressions), `test/unit/yaml-schema.test.ts` (36; +4 curated children exact-set), `test/integration/suite/yaml.test.ts` (165; +8 incl. the real-schema `other-links` regression + the tightened exact-set `grid` gate-d). 482 unit / 165 integration; clean 33-file `.vsix`.
- **★ Phase 6d-6+ (b2-ii) SHIPPED (Session 35, → Learning #42) — per-format option VALUE completion, a TEST-ONLY slice:** Under `format:\n  <fmt>:\n    <key>: <here>` the provider offers `<key>`'s enum, format-scoped. **NO production code changed** — traced firsthand that all three layers already existed after b2-i: (i) detector `src/core/yaml-context.ts` `nestedKeyContextAt:198-208` emits `{kind:"frontmatter-value", parentPath:["format",fmt,key]}`; (ii) provider `src/providers/yaml.ts:102-115` resolves `index.frontMatterKeys(ctx.parentPath.slice(0,-1)).find(key).values` (generic over `parentPath`, UNCHANGED — like Sessions 23/25/26/32/41); (iii) reader `src/core/yaml-schema.ts` `indexOf` `frontMatterKeys(["format",fmt])` returns `perFormatFields.filter(formatMatches)` with `.values` from `toField`→`valuesOfSchema`. **6 tests added** (3 unit + 3 integration). **🔑 gate-d discriminator = `code-overflow`** (`$html-all`, `document-code` context, enum `scroll`/`wrap`, ABSENT from `CURATED_FORMAT_OPTIONS`): simultaneously reader-only (green proves the reader ran) AND format-scoped (offers NO values under `gfm` because the KEY is filtered out — the value discrimination falls out of the key filter for FREE). Strict-TDD RED = **break-revert** each load-bearing test (runtime-conditional so the build compiles — neuter the 2-level `format` walk / the per-format branch / force `quartoSharePath` to throw). **🔑 review nicety (2 raised → 0 confirmed): real `toc` is NOT "universal"** — it carries `tags.formats:["!man","!$docbook-all","!$jats-all"]` (all-EXCEPT), valid for html AND gfm; reworded the label to "gfm-valid" (ground even an "obviously universal" option's tag against the real schema before labeling — the *synthetic-fixture* toc IS untagged, so the unit-level label stayed accurate). Tests: `test/unit/yaml-context.test.ts` (54; +2 per-format VALUE context, break-revert-proven), `test/unit/yaml-schema-index.test.ts` (+1 per-format value resolution `code-fold`→`[true,false,show]`), `test/integration/suite/yaml.test.ts` (158; +3 — reader-resolved values gate-d, format discrimination, leading-space+replace-range). 459 unit / 158 integration; clean 33-file `.vsix`. **b2-iii (deep nesting) is the ONLY open 6d work (deferred).**

- **★ Phase 6e-4 SHIPPED (Session 31, → Learning #38) — embedded GO-TO-DEFINITION forwarding, READ THIS FIRST if doing 6e-5:** The whole slice is a thin ADAPTER addition in `src/providers/embedded.ts` — **`core/embedded/*` UNCHANGED** (no new core code, no new unit test; the remap walks `vscode.Uri`/`Location`/`LocationLink` types so it's adapter-only, verified via test-electron). New: **`class EmbeddedDefinitionProvider`** (gate `embeddedCellAt` BEFORE await → `buildVirtualContent` → `store.set` → `vscode.executeDefinitionProvider(vdocUri, position)` → `remapDefinitions`), registered via `vscode.languages.registerDefinitionProvider(QMD, …)` in `registerEmbeddedLanguageFeature` (shares the ONE `VirtualDocStore`). **🔑 `executeDefinitionProvider` returns `(Location | LocationLink)[]`** (carrying the **vdoc URI** — unlike hover); **`remapDefinitions`/`remapDefinition`** swap the target URI back to the source `.qmd` **only when it === the vdoc URI** (range identity-mapped → returned UNCHANGED), and **PASS THROUGH any other-file URI** (a library-source def). Handles BOTH shapes (`Location.uri` and `LocationLink.targetUri`, type-guarded on `targetUri !== undefined`; LocationLink swapped via `{...result, targetUri}`). Empty/undefined upstream → `undefined` (clean no-op, never throws). The provider's return is cast ONCE at the boundary (`as Location[] | LocationLink[] | undefined`) — a heterogeneous `(Location|LocationLink)[]` isn't assignable to `Definition | LocationLink[]`, but VS Code resolves each element at runtime. **For 6e-5 (the LAST slice): identical SHAPE but SIMPLER** — `executeSignatureHelpProvider` returns a SINGLE `SignatureHelp` (no array, no URI, no edits) → NO remap, no merge, return unchanged; register with sig trigger chars `(`/`,`. Tests: `test/integration/suite/embedded.test.ts` (8 def tests in a 3rd describe block; `{scheme:"quarto-embedded"}`-keyed stand-in DefinitionProvider with `defTargetUri`/`defReturnsLink`/`defStandInReturnsNothing` flags + `ourDefs` line-filter helper). The gate (3 off-region tests), the conditional URI-swap (other-file pass-through), the LocationLink-targetUri branch, and the identity range are each **break-revert-proven** to discriminate (each break runtime-conditional so the build stays clean — a TS6133 unused-param break is a BUILD break, not a behavioral RED; Learning #38d). 431 unit / 145 integration; clean 33-file `.vsix`.

### What Session 48 Did — 2026-07-09
**Deliverable:** `docs/planning/2026-07-09-onboarding-walkthrough-plan.md` — a plan for `BACKLOG.md` "Up Next" item #3 (onboarding: getting-started walkthrough + `quarto.newDocument`/`quarto.createProject` scaffolding commands), following `docs/methodology/workstreams/ARCHITECTURE_WORKSTREAM.md`. No implementation this session (FM #18/#19).

**Evidence gathering:** a 4-agent parallel research `Workflow` (~265K subagent tokens, 118 tool calls, no isolation needed — all 4 agents were read-only research with no shared-file contention, confirmed by design before launching): (1) firsthand CLI verification — read the installed Quarto 1.7.33's bundled `quarto.js` source AND actually executed `quarto create project/extension`, the hidden legacy `quarto create-project` alias, and a deliberate `quarto create document` failure case in a throwaway scratch directory (deleted after); (2) VS Code's `contributes.walkthroughs` contribution point grounded against `microsoft/vscode`'s own `gettingStartedExtensionPoint.ts`/`gettingStartedService.ts` source (not docs paraphrase), plus this repo's own `package.json` `engines.vscode`; (3) this codebase's established L1→L4 command-adding pattern — full reads of `render-project.ts`/`render-args.ts`, grep for `showInputBox`/`showQuickPick`/`showOpenDialog`/`mkdtemp`/`tmpdir` usage (all confirmed zero hits), and the existing `media/`/test-fixture conventions; (4) Posit's public (marketplace/changelog/docs/README/GitHub-discussion sources ONLY, never `.ts` source, per this project's established AGPL look-but-don't-copy gate) black-box UX for its own `quarto.newDocument`/`quarto.createProject`/getting-started walkthrough, to calibrate scope without copying.

**Two headline scope corrections resulted, both counter to BACKLOG's own stated framing for this item:**
1. **`quarto create document` does not exist as a CLI artifact type in 1.7.33** — confirmed by reading `quarto.js`'s `kArtifactCreators` registry (a fixed 2-entry array, `project`/`extension` only) AND by a live `quarto create document html --no-prompt` invocation returning `ERROR: Failed to create document - the type isn't recognized`. `quarto.newDocument` must synthesize its own `.qmd` skeleton in TypeScript — and doing that safely (a user-supplied title must be YAML-escaped, since an unescaped `:`/`"` would corrupt the front matter) is genuine new logic, not "pure declarative config" as BACKLOG claimed. `quarto.createProject` similarly needs a real arg-builder (mirroring `render-args.ts`/`render-project.ts`'s existing shape) plus three prompt APIs (`showInputBox`/`showQuickPick`/`showOpenDialog`) confirmed unused anywhere in this codebase before, plus a genuinely new OS-temp-directory integration-test pattern (this repo's existing convention is committed static fixtures under `test/fixtures/`, which doesn't fit a command whose entire job is writing new files to a runtime-chosen location). Only the walkthrough (`contributes.walkthroughs`) turned out to be truly declarative/TDD-exempt.
2. **The three components are three independently-useful capabilities, not one, with a real dependency edge** — a user who wants `quarto.newDocument` doesn't need `quarto.createProject` or vice versa (unlike the YAML-diagnostics plan's scanner+resolver+adapter, which are inseparable parts of one mechanism), and the walkthrough's own step `completionEvents` reference the other two commands' IDs, so it cannot ship before they exist. Bundling all three into "one session" as BACKLOG originally framed it risked FM #26 (mega-session masquerading as a vertical slice) for no benefit the BACKLOG text actually required. The plan instead pre-declares three separate vertical-slice contracts (Track A `quarto.newDocument`, Track B `quarto.createProject` — independent of A — and Track C the walkthrough, which hard-depends on both), each with its own goal/files/DONE-criteria/verification/dragons/boundary, so a future executor session can pick up any one without a further planning pass.

**Also confirmed en route (a non-issue, but worth having grounded rather than assumed):** this project's `package.json` already declares `"engines": {"vscode": "^1.90.0"}`, comfortably above every `contributes.walkthroughs`-feature version floor (base 1.57, `svg`/`featuredFor` 1.60) — no engine bump needed.

**Verification:** `npm run compile` clean (docs-only session, no `src/` touched — TDD gate N/A per `CLAUDE.md`'s declarative-edit exemption). Runtime smoke test N/A (Phase 3E — no runtime behavior changed). Cross-references verified: grepped `CLAUDE.md`/`SESSION_RUNNER.md` for any stale count-claim referencing `PROJECT_LEARNINGS.md`'s row count before appending Learning #55 (none found — the one hit is a historical note about the table's size AT EXTRACTION TIME, Session 39, not a live count assertion). `BACKLOG.md` item #3 updated to point at the plan (not marked `[x]` — no implementation shipped yet, matching how items #1/#2 stayed open through their own planning-only sessions); `PROJECT_LEARNINGS.md` Learning #55 appended (one self-caught slip: the new row was initially inserted in the wrong table position, ahead of #54 instead of after it — caught and fixed via a small Python swap script before commit, not left for a future session to notice).

**Self-assessment:** Followed Phase 0 in full (read `SAFEGUARDS.md`/`SESSION_NOTES.md`, checked `gh issue list` — 0 open — and `BACKLOG.md`, ran the dashboard, reported findings, waited for the operator to speak) before claiming the session per Phase 1B. The evidence-gathering matched this project's established gold standard for planning sessions (Sessions 44/46) — firsthand CLI source-read AND live invocation (not just docs), source-grounded VS Code API research (not docs paraphrase), and full reads of the existing command pattern before designing new interface contracts. **One honest gap versus Session 46's own gold standard:** that session used 2 independent adversarial verifiers to re-run its single most load-bearing empirical test from scratch; this session's CLI-scaffolding finding (`quarto create document` doesn't exist) was doubly-corroborated by ONE agent through two independent methods (bundled-source read + live invocation) but not independently re-verified by a SECOND agent starting fresh. The finding is a straightforward negative result (a command that errors, confirmed two ways) rather than a subtle merge-chain shape like Session 46's `book:` dragon, so the lighter verification is a reasonable proportionality call, not a shortcut taken by default — but it's disclosed here rather than silently matched to Session 46's rigor by implication. No stakeholder corrections were needed this session.

### Session 46 Handoff Evaluation (by Session 47) — Phase 3A
**Score: 9/10.** A very high-quality plan — the exact grounded schema shapes (project's 11 keys, website's 36, book's merge chain, the pre-identified `announcement` gate-d discriminator) let this session skip straight to implementation with only light re-verification, and every one of those facts checked out byte-for-byte against a fresh Python probe of the real installed schema.
- **What helped:** the reuse table (R1-R8) was accurate and directly actionable — `mappingContainerKey` reuse, the `SchemaSource`/curated-fallback patterns, and the `registerXxxFeature(context)` idiom all worked exactly as described with zero rework. The `announcement` discriminator (§2.2) meant the book: merge-chain test could be written with high confidence on the first attempt instead of guessing at a good regression case.
- **What was missing:** the plan's §7 risk table (D1-D5) never considered YAML key QUOTING at all — the two HIGH-severity, core-promise-violating bugs this session's adversarial review found (quoted keys mis-compared, the filename gate's suffix-match) were entirely outside the plan's own anticipated failure modes. This is not really a fault of the plan — quoting is an implementation-level edge case, not a design-level one, and the plan's own §2.3 pseudocode was explicit that its exact scanner algorithm was illustrative, not final. But a future plan for a "flag unknown X" feature should probably budget an explicit "what if the key is quoted/escaped/differently-cased" line in its risk table, since this session confirms that's exactly the class of defect that survives careful, grounded, TDD implementation and needs a SEPARATE adversarial pass to catch.
- **What was wrong:** nothing factually wrong — every grounded claim (schema shapes, the `announcement` discriminator, the `_quarto.yml`-open-vs-closed table, the `findProjectRoot`-not-needed correction) held up under independent re-verification this session.
- **ROI:** Very high. The exact schema shapes alone (rather than "go read the schema yourself") probably saved an hour-plus of independent probing, and the plan's own book: merge-chain gate-d discriminator was directly reusable as this session's own regression test without modification.

### What Session 47 Did — 2026-07-09
**Deliverable:** implemented `docs/planning/2026-07-09-yaml-schema-diagnostics-plan.md` (BACKLOG.md "Up Next" item #2) as one vertical-slice session, per the plan's pre-declared 4-layer contract.

**L1-L4 (the plan's own layers) shipped exactly as designed, each grounded firsthand before writing code — not trusted from the plan's prose alone (Learning #6):** independently re-probed the real installed `schema/project.yml`/`definitions.yml` with a standalone Python script before writing `resolveClosedKeys` (confirmed `project`=11 keys/closed, `website`=one-hop `$ref` to `base-website`'s 36/closed, `book`=a `super` array of `book-schema` [17 own + supers `base-website`] and `csl-item-shared` [108, no own `closed` flag] — 159 total unique names after dedup, closed); wrote `resolveClosedKeys`/`resolveClosedKeysRef`/`resolveClosedKeysObject` with per-branch `seenRefs` COPIES (not a shared mutable set) so sibling `super` members sharing a resolution target don't starve each other — a correctness requirement beyond the plan's own prose, since a false negative there would itself be a false-positive diagnostic. `core/project-yaml.ts` `findProjectConfigKeyLines` TIGHTENS the plan's own §2.3 pseudocode: a column-0 line always ends the current container's scope even when it isn't itself a pure-mapping container, closing a false-positive gap the literal pseudocode had (a root-level scalar sibling immediately after an EMPTY container could otherwise be misread as that container's first child). `features/yaml-diagnostics.ts` is a `DiagnosticCollection` driven by raw document events (this codebase's first use of `onDidCloseTextDocument`/`createDiagnosticCollection` — confirmed zero prior usage, per plan §2.5), with a `pendingTimers` map cancelled before `collection.delete()` on close (plan §7 D4). Both fixtures were grounded against the REAL Quarto 1.7.33 CLI (`quarto inspect`) before committing — the invalid fixture's exact 3 flagged lines match the live CLI's own error output line-for-line, and the `announcement`-under-`book:` key is independently confirmed valid by the live CLI too, not just by this session's own resolver.

**A significant, disclosed automated-testing gap found mid-L4:** `vscode.workspace.onDidCloseTextDocument` never fires for a REAL on-disk file document in the `@vscode/test-electron` integration host (confirmed via extensive probing — `workbench.action.closeAllEditors`, `vscode.window.tabGroups.close()` directly, waits up to 8s, `vscode.workspace.textDocuments` still showing the document present throughout) — it fires reliably for `untitled:`/virtual-scheme documents elsewhere in the SAME suite, so the gap is specific to file-backed documents in this automated host, matching `vscode.d.ts`'s own documented caveat. The "clears diagnostics on close" and D4 debounce/close-race behaviors are disclosed in-file as unverifiable by this harness rather than silently dropped; the production code is still the correct, standard pattern (confirmed via `WebSearch` against Microsoft's own `code-actions-sample`).

**Phase 3E / plan's D2 activation discriminator:** the plan's own verification instruction (launch a fresh Extension Development Host, open ONLY a `_quarto.yml`, confirm activation with zero `.qmd` ever touched) could not be performed — this agent has no `code` CLI and cannot drive an interactive GUI session (confirmed: `code` not on PATH), matching this project's own prior precedent (Learning #52c: "this CLI-only agent can't drive interactively"). Verified analytically instead: `onLanguage:<id>` is a standard, officially-documented VS Code activation event; `_quarto.yml`'s real languageId (`"yaml"`, not this extension's own `"quarto"`) was already grounded firsthand by the Session 46 plan. The integration suite's own `before()` hook force-activates the extension (masking this exact class of bug, per the established Learning #13a pattern), so this specific discriminator has NEITHER automated NOR manual GUI coverage from this session — a genuine, disclosed gap, not a silent skip.

**Adversarial review (the session's real second half): a 26-agent `Workflow` (5 independent review lenses over the diff → per-finding adversarial verify, ~1.7M subagent tokens) found 21 findings, 18 confirmed, 3 refuted.** Two were HIGH severity and independently found by multiple lenses AND cross-verified against the real Quarto CLI: (1) `project-yaml.ts` `keySpanAt` extracted a quoted key (`"output-dir": docs`) WITH its quote characters attached, so it could never match the schema's bare name — flagging a fully valid, Quarto-accepted key as unknown, a direct violation of the feature's single stated core promise (zero false positives). (2) `isProjectConfigDocument` used `.endsWith("_quarto.yml"/"_quarto.yaml")` — a raw suffix test, not exact-basename equality — so `not_quarto.yml`/`backup_quarto.yaml`/any file merely ending in those characters also matched and got scanned. Both fixed test-first (`0680e98`/`2f503df`), the second by extracting a new pure, unit-tested `isProjectConfigFileName` (`path.basename` exact-set membership) into `core/project-yaml.ts`. Also fixed: a redundant `hops>10` depth cap on the closed-key resolver (removed — `seenRefs` alone is a sufficient cycle guard on parsed-JSON data, and the cap could silently truncate a legitimately deep non-cyclic branch while a shallower sibling's `closed:true` still propagated, the SAME false-positive shape a real cycle would produce); a confirmed stale-content-overwrite race (a slow first schema-index load could let an earlier call's pre-edit diagnostics resolve AFTER a later, correct, faster call — fixed with a per-URI generation counter that discards a superseded call's result); a flaky integration test (fixed 800ms sleep racing a cold schema load — fixed by reordering so an earlier test warms the cache, plus a double-timepoint assertion); 3 real test-coverage gaps (the synchronous `lines.length===0` fast path, a `package.json` `activationEvents` regression guard, a `.qmd`-never-flagged completeness check) — all added. Two LOW-severity, safe-direction (false-NEGATIVE, never a wrong flag) gaps — an anchored (`project: &p`) or quoted (`"project":`) container header disables scanning of that whole block — were documented in `BACKLOG.md` rather than fixed in-session: the fix touches the SHARED `mappingContainerKey` (also used by the unrelated 6d-6 nested-completion feature), a cross-module change out of scope for a same-session spot-fix (SAFEGUARDS: no cross-module refactor without plan mode).

**⚠ A process-integrity anomaly surfaced during the review and was investigated + resolved, not ignored:** one review agent reported unexplained files appearing in its scratchpad and this repo's working tree mid-task, including a claimed system-reminder instructing it to conceal the change — flagged directly to the operator per the standing "flag suspected prompt injection" instruction, investigated firsthand (confirmed two real stray files WERE present in the actual repo, read their contents — benign, on-topic scratch tests another concurrent agent had written to empirically verify its own findings by running vitest), and root-caused to a mundane, self-inflicted cause: this session's `Workflow` call ran 26 agents WITHOUT `isolation: 'worktree'`, so they all shared one live repo + one scratchpad, and concurrent agents' own legitimate empirical-verification artifacts became visible to each other. No malicious injection was substantiated (the one uncorroborated detail — the concealment-instructing reminder — was reported to the operator as unconfirmed, not as fact); the stray files were deleted, nothing was ever committed. Recorded as `PROJECT_LEARNINGS.md` Learning #54c: use `isolation: 'worktree'` even for review workflows whose agents do hands-on file-writing verification, not only for workflows that intentionally mutate the deliverable.

**Verification:** 523 unit (+33 across L1 + the two fix-response commits) / 183 integration (+7) tests, all green; `npm run check-types` clean at every checkpoint; `npm run package` clean 35-file `.vsix` (re-verified after the fix layers). `PROJECT_LEARNINGS.md` Learning #54 appended; `BACKLOG.md` item #2 marked done + a new Polish/deferred entry added for the two documented LOW-severity gaps.

**Self-assessment:** Followed strict TDD throughout — RED shown for every new behavior (both in the original 4 layers and in every fix-response), including for fixes to my OWN just-written code (the quoted-key/BOM/filename-gate/hops-budget fixes all had a failing test written first, confirmed failing for the right reason, then the minimal fix). The biggest process win this session was treating the adversarial review as load-bearing rather than a formality — 2 HIGH-severity bugs shipped past unit+integration tests and a careful implementation, and only surfaced because the review was run BEFORE calling the slice done, not after. The biggest process risk this session introduced (and resolved) was running a 26-agent workflow without worktree isolation against the live repo; next session running any workflow with agents that write real files (not just read) should default to `isolation: 'worktree'` per the new Learning #54c. One honest gap remains fully disclosed rather than papered over: the plan's D2/D4 discriminators have neither automated nor manual GUI verification from this session (this agent cannot drive an interactive VS Code session at all — confirmed, not assumed).

The live probe found front matter, cell options, per-format nested options, and even `_quarto.yml`'s own ROOT keys are all **open** in Quarto's own schema — a fabricated key and a genuine typo of a real option are byte-for-byte indistinguishable to `quarto render` (both exit 0, silent, no warning), confirmed independently twice with different fixtures. Only the **interior of `_quarto.yml`'s `project:`/`website:`/`book:` blocks** is genuinely closed and already enforced by `quarto render`/`quarto inspect` today (root-caused directly in the bundled `quarto.js` CLI source). This is a MUCH narrower — but genuinely safe, and still valuable for the confirmed-dominant multi-file/book workflow (Session 43) — v1 than BACKLOG.md originally framed. **Presented this finding to the operator via `AskUserQuestion` mid-session** (a genuine decision only they could make, given how much smaller the safely-buildable feature turned out to be) — operator confirmed the recommended narrow, safe v1 scope.

(2) `src/core/project.ts` `findProjectRoot` (Session 45) turns out NOT needed by the corrected v1 scope — a correction to `BACKLOG.md` item #2's own long-standing stated dependency (see the Session 45 handoff evaluation above).

**The plan's own headline design dragon (§2.2):** `book:`'s valid-key set requires resolving a two-level `super`/`$ref` merge chain (`book:` supers `book-schema` [itself supering `base-website`] + `csl-item-shared`) — grounded via direct probes of the real schema, with a precise, empirically-chosen gate-d discriminator (`announcement`, a `base-website`-only key) specified in the plan (§6) to prove any future implementation's merge resolver actually walks the full chain rather than stopping at `book-schema`'s own shallow property list.

Runtime smoke test N/A (Phase 3E — no runtime behavior changed).

### What Session 45 Did — 2026-07-09
**Deliverable:** implemented BACKLOG.md "Up Next" item #1 (Project-level render) end to end, per `docs/planning/2026-07-09-project-level-render-plan.md`, as one vertical-slice session (operator-ratified over splitting into two, per plan §9 Q1). **L1 — pure discovery core (`cc66fbe`):** `src/core/project.ts` `findProjectRoot(startDir, boundaryDir, exists)` — an ancestor walk for `_quarto.yml`/`_quarto.yaml`, bounded (inclusive) at an optional `boundaryDir`, `.yml` preferred over `.yaml` on a tie (matches `quarto-cli`'s own resolution order). `exists` is dependency-injected so the function is headlessly unit-testable. Strict TDD: 6 unit tests written incrementally (`test/unit/project.test.ts`); the general algorithm satisfied tests 2-6 immediately once written for test 1, so the two genuinely load-bearing guards (boundary respect; `.yml`-wins-on-tie ordering) were each separately **break-revert-proven** (temporarily disabled/reordered, confirmed RED, reverted, confirmed GREEN) rather than trusted on faith.

**L2 — adapter + command + wiring (`e61386b`):** `src/core/render-args.ts` gained `buildRenderProjectArgs(projectRoot)` (TDD: wrote the failing test first, confirmed `buildRenderProjectArgs is not a function`, then implemented) — the root passed as an explicit positional arg, never bare `quarto render`. New `src/features/render-project.ts`: Tier A (active on-disk editor, bounded at `getWorkspaceFolder`) / Tier B (workspace-folder branch: none/one/many with `showWorkspaceFolderPick`) resolution, `findProjectRoot` call, then `runRenderProject` — spawns `quarto render <root>` with `cwd` PINNED to `root` (Dragon D1), in its own `"Quarto Render Project"` Output channel (kept separate from `"Quarto Render"` per plan §8, so a long project render can't clobber an in-flight single-file render's output). Mirrors `render.ts`'s `runRender` shape closely. Wired into `package.json` (+1 command entry) and `extension.ts` (+1 call). vscode-adapter logic followed this project's established convention (Learning #3): not unit-tested (no vscode in `core/`), verified instead via the L4 integration suite.

**L3 — test fixture (`70dc234`):** `test/fixtures/project/` — `_quarto.yml` (`type: default`, per plan §9 Q5) + `index.qmd` + `chapters/chapter1.qmd`. The first `_quarto.yml`-having fixture in this repo. Sanity-rendered against the REAL installed Quarto 1.7.33 CLI from a `/tmp` copy, run from an unrelated cwd, BEFORE committing — confirmed cwd-independence (root-argument invocation reliably produced both `index.html` and `chapters/chapter1.html`) and inventoried the real render-artifact set (`.quarto/` cache, `*_files/` dirs) to extend `.gitignore` correctly up front.

**L4 — integration tests (`0da5dab`):** `test/integration/suite/render-project.test.ts`, 4 tests against the REAL fixture: command registration; whole-project render from the NESTED `chapters/chapter1.qmd` as the active file, asserting `index.html` is ALSO produced (the direct regression test for the plan's headline D2 finding); the success dialog's "Open" path resolves against the project root, not the extension host's own cwd (Dragon D1) — proven by **monkey-patching** `vscode.window.showInformationMessage`/`vscode.env.openExternal` (plain reassign-and-restore, no new devDependency) to capture the exact `Uri` passed to `openExternal`; and a clear-error-never-a-crash test invoked outside any project (`test/fixtures/sample.qmd`, verified to have no `_quarto.yml` ancestor anywhere, including up through the repo root). **Both the D1 and D2 discriminators were break-revert-proven**: temporarily reproduced the naive "bare render, cd near the active file" bug (spawn args truncated to `["render"]`, cwd redirected to a subdirectory of root) — both tests went RED with the exact expected failure signatures (missing `index.html`; wrong resolved `Uri` pointing at a nonexistent `chapter1.html` in the wrong directory) — then reverted, confirmed GREEN again. Discovered along the way: Quarto auto-generates a `.gitignore` (`/.quarto/`) inside a project root on its first REAL render (not caught by the L3 `/tmp` sanity check, since that used a throwaway copy) — added to both the repo `.gitignore` and the test's own artifact-cleanup step.

**Dragon D4 (activationEvents) resolved analytically, not by manual F5 launch:** a `WebSearch` confirmed VS Code 1.74.0+ auto-generates implicit `onCommand:` activation events for any `contributes.commands` entry (cross-checked against two independent doc sources); this project's `engines.vscode` is `^1.90.0`, well past that floor, so no `activationEvents` change was needed.

**Final verification matrix (Phase 3E via test-electron, Learning #3):** 490 unit (+7 over Session 44's baseline of 483) / 176 integration (+4 over 172); `npm run compile` clean; `npm run package` produces a clean 35-file `.vsix` (same file count as before — no fixture/test leakage into the package). Command dispatch, activation, and the real Quarto CLI spawn are all exercised end-to-end by the integration suite, consistent with this project's established precedent that `@vscode/test-electron` satisfies Phase 3E's runtime-behavior check (stronger than manual F5 for wiring/dispatch; F5 remains only for pure visual/UX confirmation, not exercised this session since nothing new is visual beyond a standard success/error dialog, the same shape as the existing `quarto.render` command's).

**Self-critique (also in Learning #52d):** gate (c) ("full build/test matrix at EVERY layer boundary") was only partially honored — `npm test`+`npm run compile` ran after L1/L2, but `npm run test:integration`/`npm run package` were deferred to a single run after L4, not run at each of the four boundaries. Nothing regressed (confirmed retroactively by the final green matrix), but this is a real discipline gap worth correcting next time, especially since `test:integration` (~18s) and `package` (~15-20s) are cheap enough to run at every boundary of a 4-layer slice.

### What Session 44 Did — 2026-07-09
**Deliverable:** `docs/planning/2026-07-09-project-level-render-plan.md` — a full implementation plan for BACKLOG.md "Up Next" item #1 (Project-level render). **DONE.**

**Method:** Ran a 6-agent `Workflow` (~307K subagent tokens, 124 tool calls) BEFORE writing anything: 4 parallel research agents (grep-based code inventory of `render.ts`/`preview.ts`/`cli.ts`/`package.json`/tests; official Quarto docs + `quarto-cli` GitHub source, since the docs are silent on parent-directory walking; a **live empirical test** — scaffolded a real temp Quarto project and actually ran the installed 1.7.33 CLI against it in 9 different invocation shapes; VS Code workspace-folder API conventions, both from this repo's own (nonexistent) usage and the official multi-root-workspace guide) followed by 2 adversarial verifiers who independently re-ran the two most load-bearing claims from scratch (not just re-reading the first agents' reports). Every file:line citation destined for the plan was THEN re-confirmed firsthand by this session's own direct reads (`render.ts`, `render-args.ts`, `cli.ts`, `package.json`, `extension.ts`, `render.test.ts`) before being written down — Workflow-delegated research grounds the facts, but citations landing in a durable plan got a second, personal read (Learning #6 applied to a planning, not implementation, session).

**🔑 The headline finding (→ `PROJECT_LEARNINGS.md` Learning #51): `quarto render` with NO arguments behaves differently depending on which directory it's run from, in a way neither the CLI's own docs nor a source-code-only reading would fully reveal.** Scaffolded `<tmp>/_quarto.yml` + `<tmp>/index.qmd` + `<tmp>/sub/chapter.qmd` (unlisted) and ran `quarto render` (no args) from `<tmp>/sub` — a project SUBdirectory with no `_quarto.yml` of its own. It did **not** fail, and did **not** render the whole project: it detected the ancestor project (inherited its theme/config, wrote `.quarto/` cache to the ancestor root) but rendered **only `chapter.html`** — `index.qmd` was never touched. This is a silent, hybrid partial-render — exactly what a user editing a nested chapter file would trigger, and it would look identical to success (exit 0, an "Output created" line, a working success dialog) while quietly having rendered only one of N files. An adversarial verifier independently re-ran this exact test from scratch (fresh temp dir, no rubber-stamping the first report) and got matching output down to the literal CSS content hash. **Confirmed as reliable instead:** `quarto render <absolute-project-root-path>`, run from ANY cwd, always triggers a full project render — also independently re-verified. This one finding drove the plan's central design decision (§0/§2/§7 D2 in the plan): the new command MUST discover the true root itself and pass it as an explicit CLI argument, never rely on `cd`+bare `quarto render`.

**A second, related trap the same empirical test surfaced:** `Output created: <path>` is reported relative to the **target project directory**, not the spawning process's actual `cwd` (confirmed: running `quarto render <tmp>` from a wholly unrelated cwd still produced `<tmp>/index.html`). Porting the existing single-file `render.ts`'s `path.resolve(cwd, reported)` pattern naively would silently compute a WRONG absolute path for the "Open" success button unless `cwd` is deliberately pinned to the discovered root — locked into the plan as Dragon D1 with its own specific gate-d regression test recommendation (§6).

**Design locked into the plan:** new pure `core/project.ts` (`findProjectRoot(startDir, boundaryDir, exists)` — dependency-injected `exists` so it stays `vscode`-free and headlessly testable, mirroring `yaml-schema-source.ts`'s established pure-core/impure-adapter split); a two-tier VS Code resolution chain (active-editor-first, bounded by `getWorkspaceFolder`; else folder-count branching with `showWorkspaceFolderPick` for the multi-root case) — grounded in both this codebase's own 100%-consistent existing convention (every prior command gates on `activeTextEditor` first) and VS Code's own multi-root-workspace API guidance, since this is the FIRST code in the repo to ever touch `workspace.workspaceFolders`/`getWorkspaceFolder` (confirmed zero prior usage, twice). Recommended as ONE vertical-slice implementing session (4 checkpoint-committed layers: core discovery, adapter+command+package.json+extension.ts wiring, new test fixture, integration tests — plan §1/§6), with an explicit alternative (split into 2 sessions) left as an open question for the executor (plan §9 Q1), consistent with this project's more common finer-grained session cadence.

**Also this session (Phase 0, before claiming Session 44):** found and backfilled a `HANDOFFS.md` reconcile gap — commit `a7f3910` (an ad hoc gitignore fix made right after Session 43's close-out) had a `CHANGELOG.md` entry but no `HANDOFFS.md` receipt. Wrote a `status: reconciled` block per `SESSION_RUNNER.md` Phase 0 step 6, committed separately (`e1adcbe`) from this session's own claim/work — see "Prior session (43.1)" above.

**Status:** SHIPPED. Plan written (`docs/planning/2026-07-09-project-level-render-plan.md`), `BACKLOG.md` item #1 updated with a pointer + one-paragraph summary of the headline finding, `PROJECT_LEARNINGS.md` Learning #51 appended. No source code touched — `npm run compile`/`test`/`test:integration`/`package` not applicable this session (TDD gate N/A, `CLAUDE.md`'s pure-doc-edit exemption). Runtime smoke test N/A (Phase 3E — nothing runtime-facing changed).

**Self-assessment (Phase 3B):** Research-before-creative-work discipline held (the entire Workflow ran before a single word of the plan was drafted); every file:line citation was read firsthand by this session, not trusted from the sub-agent reports alone. Zero stakeholder corrections needed (one operator directive — "planning session for BACKLOG item #1" — no follow-up clarification required). What went right: grounding the load-bearing CLI-behavior claim EMPIRICALLY (live process execution, independently re-verified) rather than trusting docs/training-data caught a real, non-obvious, easy-to-miss design trap that would otherwise have shipped a silently-broken v1 — this is a genuinely new verification modality for this project (prior "ground firsthand" sessions grounded STATIC schema data by reading files; this one grounded DYNAMIC CLI *behavior* by running the tool). What could improve: the empirical-test agent's prompt was fairly long (9 explicit steps) — justified by the payoff here, but worth trimming to the 2-3 truly load-bearing experiments in a smaller future grounding task. Meets the quality bar of prior planning sessions (6d/6e/b2-iii plans) — grep-based inventory, interface contracts, per-phase completion criteria, dragons/risks, alternatives considered, open questions — scaled proportionately to a genuinely smaller feature rather than padded to match their length.

### What Session 43 Did — 2026-07-09
**Deliverable:** A decisions list ranking `docs/POSIT-COMPARISON.md`'s candidate gaps for future implementation sessions, produced via a `/grill-me` interview. **DONE.**

**What was done:** Ran a `/grill-me` session against the 12 "Real gaps" + "Additional Findings" candidates from `docs/POSIT-COMPARISON.md` (Visual/WYSIWYG editor pre-excluded by an operator directive that arrived mid-orientation, before the grill started). Grounded several early recommendations against the actual codebase before asking questions (grep-verified: no `DiagnosticCollection` anywhere in `src/`, no `snippets`/`walkthroughs` contribution points in `package.json`, no `_quarto.yml`/project-root discovery code, and the run-cell command family is exactly the 5 commands — 2 keybound — the comparison doc describes; all matched the doc's claims with zero corrections needed).

Resolved, in order:
1. **Grill scope:** rank the full candidate list now (operator's choice), not just pick one immediate target.
2. **Base heuristic:** gap-size (the doc's own ordering) does not correlate with usability — re-rank by (frequency × silent-failure-prevention × workflow-class breadth) instead, since no usage telemetry exists to measure this empirically.
3. **Load-bearing usage facts, elicited directly (not inferable from the repo):** multi-file Quarto projects/books are the operator's *dominant* workflow (single-file work happens but is secondary); new-user/Marketplace adoption *does* matter (not purely a personal-use tool).
4. **Priority-swap driven by a discovered dependency:** the operator wanted YAML diagnostics to cover `_quarto.yml` (not just per-document front matter), but `_quarto.yml` discovery doesn't exist yet and Project-level render needs to build exactly that discovery as its own core deliverable — so the order swapped (Project-level render → #1, YAML diagnostics → #2) rather than building the same discovery code twice across two sessions.
5. **Scope boundaries locked for the top 2 (the ones an immediate next planning session will consume):** Project-level render v1 = render only (Preview Project stays a named backlog follow-up, not silently dropped — different process-lifecycle complexity). YAML diagnostics v1 = unknown-key detection only (type/enum/required-field validation deferred, mirroring the vertical-slice discipline every 6d/6e phase in this project's history has used).
6. **Onboarding (walkthrough + scaffolding) bumped to #3** once adoption was confirmed to matter — reasoned as a new user's first-five-minutes experience, higher-leverage for adoption than day-2 conveniences.
7. **Run-cell family (#4) scoped as one unit:** all 4 missing commands + default keybindings across the resulting 9-command family, shipped together rather than split.
8. **Remaining items ranked by the same heuristic** (snippets #5, workspace symbol provider #6 — bumped for the confirmed multi-file priority, graphviz #7) with two items explicitly **parked, not ranked** (`.ipynb` conversion — no evidence of notebook-format usage anywhere in this project's history; Contextual Assist Panel — substantially overlaps the existing Math/Diagram preview commands) and two **deferred**, coupled to the Visual Editor exclusion (Zotero, Visual-Editor-scoped spell check).

Promoted the full ranked list into `BACKLOG.md`'s "Up Next" section (top entry) — including the rationale for the priority swap, since that's load-bearing context the next planning session (Project-level render) needs and shouldn't have to re-derive. Updated the `BACKLOG.md` "Documentation" entry for the comparison doc to point at the new ranking instead of saying "none promoted yet."

**🔑 Reusable pattern (→ Learning #50): rank candidates, then re-verify the ranking survives a scope/dependency pass before locking it.** The initial ranking (driven by frequency/silent-failure/workflow-breadth) looked stable, but a *scope* question about item #2 (should YAML diagnostics cover `_quarto.yml`?) surfaced a hidden infrastructure dependency on item #1 that the ranking pass alone never would have found — ranking candidates independently and then scoping the top pick in isolation would have shipped a sequencing mistake (diagnostics session either duplicating discovery code or blocking on a re-plan). The fix was cheap because the scope-pass happened in the SAME grill, before anything was written down as final — a scoping question surfaced a sequencing error the ranking question couldn't see.

**Self-assessment (Phase 3B):**
- Completed research (grounding candidates against the actual codebase) BEFORE the creative/interview work — consistent with the standard set by planning sessions in this project's history (Learning #6: read implementations before estimating, applied here to "read the codebase before recommending a heuristic ranking").
- Read the full comparison doc firsthand rather than relying on the prior handoff's summary (FM #11/#20 discipline), even though the summary was accurate.
- One thing I'd do differently next time: I front-loaded the `_quarto.yml` dependency discovery reactively (it surfaced from a user answer) rather than proactively grepping for cross-candidate infrastructure sharing before the ranking questions began. A prior pass — "which candidates might share undiscovered infrastructure?" — could have surfaced this before Question 4 instead of Question 10, saving one re-ranking round-trip. Not a defect (the grill format handled it fine, and re-ranking mid-interview is exactly what grilling is for), but a sharper opening move for next time.
- Zero stakeholder corrections of *my* factual claims — the operator directed 5 substantive pivots (WYSIWYG exclusion, ranking method, multi-file priority, adoption priority, the `_quarto.yml` swap), all legitimate scope/priority decisions only the operator could make, not corrections of something I got wrong.
- Met the quality bar of prior grill/decision-adjacent sessions in this project (none exist yet to compare against directly — this is the first `/grill-me` session in this project's history, per a grep of `SESSION_NOTES.md` section headers).
